The ministry's infrastructure has been handed over to the GATAB for forty-nine years through a protocol for free intifa rights. The project includes drinking and using water, sewage and treatment facilities, deep sea discharges, garbage collection and recycling facilities, and anti-harmal services, tourism promotions of the service area by GATAB. The GATAB provides its subscribers with the financial resources it needs each year from its subscribers, in exchange for its drinking and using water (canalization) service. The activities of Yusuf Üras, the AKP's mayor of Belt Tekirova Belde, who has ruled the GATAB for seven years have not been investigated. The 2011 budget of GATAB, established to provide infrastructure, sewers, garbage and exterminating services, is 49.5 million liras. Only $4.6m of this budget is claimed to be spent on infrastructure. The remaining 45 million liras are unknown. The relevant budget is claimed to be spent on illegal foreign flights, tenders, rental vehicles, cell phones for relatives, promotions that are made at times the cost of subservients, improper projects. Other than that, dozens of allegations of corruption that we can't count here have now become very clear. Not to mention the money the president and his followers took from the institution's vault. President Yusuf Üras has officially emptied the safe for his trips abroad. We've become allies and advisors, and they've taken a lot of money from the vault, using the lame business as an excuse. The actions were not limited to these. It's made up by making it look like $10 for the jobs that can be done for a dollar. The greenhouse windows, the pots, the earth, the rat poison, the pens seem to have been paid a lot of money. At the same time, outside service purchases, subcontractors have served as government officials on the GATAB tender commission. It's also necessary to examine the records of such an institution. There are warehouses in the Belt district of Antalya where records and documents are kept. We heard that something was going to happen to these warehouses at the beginning of the summer, and we warned the authorities through the press, but our warnings were ignored. Soon there was a fire near the warehouses. Some of the relevant warehouses were said to have been damaged by the fire, but the records of the GATAB have been unaccounted for. There is no way to address an institution that has a $50m annual budget and whose allegations of corruption have become public. The Ministry of Interior does not conduct a proper inspection of this institution. Despite all complaints, the governorship does not oversee the institution. Although the Belt Co-ordinator has repeatedly requested the records and documents of the institution, he has been moved to the center several times, but has been reinstated by court decrees. A review of such an institution is important to the TBMM to ensure that our citizens' money is not squandered. The following grounds would be appropriate for a parliamentary investigation into corruption in the South Antalya Tourism Development and Infrastructure Union (GATAB) and to determine it by our Grand Assembly. tbmm-2015-02-26sit05spe0208par0327	Mr. President, I have received a pledge on behalf of the People's Democratic Party on behalf of the Republican People's Party's Group's proposal. I salute the General Assembly. According to 2011 data, the population of Turkey consists of 24 million children within the age of 0-18. What we're talking about is a matter of the population of 24 million people. If the government's current policies and sense of security continues this way, all of these children are potentially likely to be detained, court-martialed, and incarceration. We see and hear and read what children go through every day in the streets, detention and prisons. Imagine what it would do to children in prison in broad daylight above the 12-year-old Nihath, in broad daylight, with armoured vehicles, with rifles, with shotguns, with gas, with blind doors, dumb walls, and servants without conscience. Mr. President, high-value lawmakers say that the situation of childhood should be handled as the most sensitive and special condition of the human being, and that in any business and procedure related to children, such sensitivity and child nature should be treated accordingly. The countries with high standards of democracy and human development organise laws, legal legislation and justice systems, considering this particular situation of children. In the United Nations and the European Union, where Turkey is a part of it, they have drawn up a number of special arrangements for children and opened them to state signatures. Turkey signed a total of 27 documents, which are binding on the rights of children, stating that it will adhere to the agreements and will meet its requirements in civil law. However, in Turkey, more than 300,000 children meet security units each year. More than 10,000 children a year are subject to detention and prison implementation. According to data published by the Ministry of Justice in January 2015, there are 100 different types of children's prisons, including three of them in prison. A total of 2,057 children remain in prisons where 542 have been convicted and 1,515 have been imprisoned. The Department of Justice's separation of age groups between 12 and 17 and 18-20 is a grouping aimed at concealing the number of children in prison. The ministry grouped 7.638 people as 18-20 years old. According to this group, the 17-year-olds are considered minors, while the law considers the 18-year-olds to be minors. In addition, 450 children in age 0-6 remain in prison with their mother because of necessity. In 27 agreements that I have stated that Turkey has also signed, the decision to detain and put in prison for children entering the justice system can be applied as an exception and a last resort. On the contrary in Turkey, the above data shows that the decision to be taken into custody for children and put in prison is considered the first and only option. The rights of many children, even the right to live outside, are on the agenda where they are exposed by their peers in prisons, both by public officials and by their peers, to the extent that people's dignity and community conscience have been harmed. Sincan, Shakran, Van and Ceyhan are responsible for the inhumane practices of children in prisons, the security mentality that sees children as potential criminals outside and inside, and shuts them in prisons. The torture, harassment, and rape allegations of children in prisons continue to be reprocessed because this security mentality and security judiciary is not actively investigated. Instead of investigating the allegations of children who have somehow managed to expose their abuse to the press and the public, exemplary cases are being filed against the children for defaming the authorities.
